Let's design a robust local optimizer and hope. Since we can't run, need choose parameters conservative. We can also include many simple seeds and a deterministic hill-climbing that uses exact direct/FFT to improve. The code itself will perform search; even if my manual predictions fail, it may find improvement. Need ensure it doesn't hang or crash.

Let's outline final program architecture:
```
import numpy as np, time

def _fft_len(n): return 1 << (2*n-2).bit_length()

def convolve_fft(a, L=None):
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=p2(2*n-1)
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]

def conv_hankle_with_W(p, W, L):
    # returns S_i=Σ_j p_j W_{i+j}
    pr=p[::-1]
    fa=np.fft.rfft(pr,L); fw=np.fft.rfft(W,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,L)
    return c[n-1:2*n-1]
```
Need L for p(W) convolution length n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2; if using same L for p and W? p length n, W length 2n-1, FFT length >=3n-2. Our earlier y convolution uses L_y >=2n-1, but Hankel gradient needs L_h >=3n-2. Choose L = next_power_of_two(3*n-2) or maybe next pow 4n for simplicity. For n=6121, 3n~18360, pow32768. Good. Use one L for all.

For y: p length n, convolve_fft with L (valid length 2n-1) from fa*fa. Using same L okay.

Objective: p positive sum1. Compute y = n * conv[:2n-1]. Due FFT errors, clip small negative 0. max_y = y.max(). score=2*max_y? Wait if p sum1, score =2n max(c)/(1)^2 = 2 max(n c)=2 max_y. Good. But if p sum not exactly 1 due numerical, y should scale by sum²? We'll normalize p; if S not 1, score=2 max(n c)/S² *? Actually c sum scale; y = n c / S²? Since p probabilities normalized S=1. We'll normalize after every update.

Use initial p from seeds. We can evaluate exact with evaluate_sequence on candidate list for best, but optimize with y.

Seed generation functions top-level:
- uniform
- exp_decay: p = exp(-λ x), normalized, λ vary.
- exp_rise: exp(λ x)
- power: x^a for a in [0.2,0.5,0.8,1,1.5], normalized; for x[0]=0 issue use x+1e-12 then normalize.
- reciprocal: 1/(B+x) B in [0.2,0.5,1,2]
- beta-ish: x^a(1-x)^b
- polynomial: (1+cos/sin) with nonnegative rand? We'll include deterministic set.
- random phases: Let Lfft, choose random phases with conjugate symmetry, inverse FFT real, take abs, normalize; maybe this yields low-peak convolution? Generate several.
- use height_sequence_1 if available: clip normalize; also its reversed, smoothed.

Then run local optimization on each seed perhaps a few seconds. Use time budget.

Local optimizer function `optimize_sequence(p, budget)`:
- Work in probability p (float64). Maintain z = log(p) maybe with initial; but p may have zeros from seeds? We'll add small floor: p = p + eps (1/n), normalize.
- Adam state m,v for z length n. Or directly use log p. We use z=log(p). At each step:
  1. p = softmax(z) / sum? We can compute z normalized; but after many updates z range may drift. We can periodically set z=log(p).
  2. y = n(conv p).
  3. W_max = softmax(γ y) (stable). Maybe also W_flat = y - avg (avg=n/(2*n-1)). W=W_max + flat_weight*W_flat.
  But note W_flat can be negative. If W has negative, convolution g may negative? That's fine. Need scale. The gradient for F = LSE + λ*0.5Σ(y-avg)^2: W_max (sum1) + λ*(y-avg). We'll set λ (flat_weight) maybe 0.2? We can tune over schedule: start 0.5, decay to 0.1? Actually L2 may help refine, but pure LSE for max. Use λ = 0.2 initially. Also we can rescale W: W = W_max + λ*(y-avg) / max_y? To keep same scale. y-avg O0.5. λ=0.2 gives comparable to softmax weights (which sum to1, individual maybe ~1/n? Wait softmax over 2n weights sums1, each peak ~1/active; if γ moderate, peak weight maybe 1/n to 1. y-avg values O0.5. λ=0.2 gives 0.1, comparable. Good.
  However g_p=2n sum W p; if W_max sum1, g_p ~O(2n * p avg) ~O(2) as before. W_flat sum zero, can be negative; okay.
  4. Compute S=conv_hankle(p, W, L_h). g_p=2n*S.
  5. Gradient wrt z: g_z = p*(g_p - np.dot(p, g_p)). We can scale: g_z *= n (or maybe use as is with lr). Use n scaling to make updates O(1). Let grad = n*g_z.
  6. Adam: m=β1*m+(1-β1)grad; v=β2*v+(1-β2)grad²; mhat, vhat. z = z - lr * mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps). For non-convex with varying γ, Adam may oscillate. Use lr maybe 0.05 to 0.2? Since grad n*g_z: uniform p, g_z~p*(g_p-μ)~(1/n)*O(1), n*g_z~O(1). lr 0.05 yields step 0.05 in log; reasonable. Use lr schedule: start 0.1, decay to 0.02. Or adaptive: if max_y improves keep, else reduce lr.
  7. Re-normalize p=softmax(z). To avoid underflow: z = z - max(z) before exp. Also periodic recenter z -= mean(z) (or weighted? mean) and set p=softmax.
  8. Compute max_y. Keep best if < best_y - tol. Periodically exact evaluate_sequence every maybe 1s or only when improvement >1e-5, but exact direct costly. We can limit exact evals (e.g., 50) for best candidates to verify. But final returned will be evaluated; search can rely on FFT with tiny error. To be safe, before returning, exact evaluate best candidate and maybe direct local tweaks.

- Acceptance: Since we use Adam, update z always. But to avoid wandering, we can do line search/trials: For each iteration, propose new_z; compute y_new? That doubles cost. Maybe just use gradient; if max_y starts increasing, reduce lr or restart. We can maintain best_p and if current degrades > threshold for many steps, revert to best_p. Alternative simpler optimization: Use FISTA-like multiplicative updates in p-space:
```
for iter:
  y = ...
  W = softmax(gamma*y)
  S = hankel(p,W)
  g = S  # >=0
  p_new = p / (1 + eta * g) ???
```
Which update? If want reduce peaks, p_i should be lower where high diagonals involving i. The gradient g_p_i = 2n S_i. Since F = logsumexp, gradient positive. Conservation: multiply p_i by exp(-α g_p_i) and normalize. This is simple and stable; no z gradient complications. Let's examine with uniform p and W softmax center: g_i large in middle? Multiplicative lowers middle, raises edges. Might find skew or U-shaped. We can use adaptive α. Since g_i scales O(1? Actually S_i sum W p ~? W sum1, p sum1; S_i ≈1/n if W distributed; 2n S_i≈2). Multiplicative exp(-α*2) per step. α=0.05. This might be efficient. No Adam state. Can also add flat_weight to W making g can be negative; exp(positive) increases. Maybe use `p = normalize(p * np.exp(-eta * (g - g.mean())))`? If g nonnegative only, normalization handles mean; subtract mean not necessary for relative but helps avoid all decrease (no effect after norm). Use g centered: p *= exp(-η*(g - g.min()?) or -η*(g - g.mean())). For nonnegative, centering changes relative but not total; using mean may allow small g increase. For flat term, use raw g centered? Since after normalization constant shift irrelevant, can compute `rel = g - np.mean(g)` (arithmetic) for symmetric log update. This equivalent to gradient with arithmetic Lagrange, not exact but okay.

Could use Adam on this multiplicative update: maintain velocity for z updates = -η*rel.

Maybe simpler: Randomly sample α in [0.01,0.1] for line search? We can do a deterministic update and accept if improves.

Potential speed: Each iteration needs two FFTs (y and hankel) plus irffts; L=32768, very fast (~0.01s?). In Python, numpy FFT 32768 complex ~0.1ms? Maybe 1ms. 100k iterations possible in 2400s? With Python overhead maybe 1000/s? Actually FFT 32768 ~0.2ms in C, Python loops overhead 0.05ms, total ~0.3ms, 10k/s. 100k iterations. Good.

But exact evaluate_sequence direct O(n²) slow; avoid many.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start_time=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start + max(0,budget_s-10)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # choose n
    n=0
    try: n=len(height_sequence_1)
    except: n=4096
    if n<128: n=4096
    # maybe cap at 8192 to keep eval feasible. If len global 6121 use that; else 4096? Target n maybe 6121. Use n=min(max(n,2048),8192) maybe 6121.
    L=1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
    best_p=None; best_y=np.inf; best_exact=np.inf
    seeds = [...]
    for seed_info in seeds:
       p=seed...
       if time left:
          p2,y2=optimize(p, time_slice)
          evaluate if y2 below current
    # final exact local search with direct conv for best few
    return best_p.tolist()
```

`evaluate_y(p, L)`:

fa=np.fft.rfft(p,L)

y = c[:2*n-1] * n
np.maximum(y,0,out=y) # due errors
return y
```

`hankel_conv(p, W, L)` as above:
```
pr = p[::-1]
fa=np.fft.rfft(pr,L)
fw=np.fft.rfft(W,L)
c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,L)
return c[n-1:2*n-1]
```

Adam optimizer:
```
def optimize_seed(p0, gamma_start, gamma_end, lr_start, lr_end, flat_w, time_limit):
   n=p0.size
   p = p0.copy(); p = np.clip(p,0,None); p += 1e-12; p/=p.sum()
   z = np.log(p) - np.log(n)
   m = np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n)
   beta1=0.9; beta2=0.999; eps=1e-8
   best_p=p.copy(); best_y=np.inf; best_score? y max.
   stall=0
   last_exact=0
   it=0
   while time < limit:
      # schedule parameters
      tfrac = min(1, elapsed/time_limit)
      gamma = gamma_start + (gamma_end-gamma_start)*tfrac
      lr = lr_start + (lr_end-lr_start)*tfrac
      flat = flat_w maybe
      y = get_y(p)
      cur = max_y(y)
      if cur < best_y - 1e-8: best=cur; best_p=p.copy()
         maybe exact evaluate if time since last > 5
      # compute W
      Wl = softmax_gamma_y(y, gamma)
      W = Wl + flat*(y - avg)
      # if lots negative maybe clip? Gradient can negative, okay. But for log update, if W too negative, update may increase some p too much. Keep W finite; maybe clip to [-C,C]? Use raw.
      S = hankel_conv(p,W,L)
      g = 2*n*S
      mu = np.dot(p,g)
      grad = n * p * (g - mu) # exact softmax gradient scaled
      # Adam
      m = (1-beta1)*grad + beta1*m
      v = (1-beta2)*grad*grad + beta2*v
      mhat = m/(1-beta1**(it+1))
      vhat = v/(1-beta2**(it+1))
      z = z - lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
      # Normalize z? Since softmax invariant to constant shift, but for log range:
      z -= np.mean(z)  # keeps around 0? But doesn't preserve p? subtracting mean changes p by constant? softmax invariant to constant, yes.
      # If range large, clip z within [-50,50]? 
      z = np.clip(z, -50, 50)
      p_new = softmax(z)
      # Maybe line search: if max_y(p_new) not too bad? We can compute; but it's next loop.
      p = p_new
      it+=1
   return best_p,best_y
```

Need implement resampling probabilities. Given p_small length m, we want p_large length n preserving shape. Use CDF linear interpolation: x_small points at i/(m-1), cdf_small cumulative; for x_large = j/(n-1), value = inverse CDF. Compute p_large = diff of interp values. Or simpler: use FFT upsampling? CDF method:
```
def resample_p(p_small, n):
    cs_small = np.cumsum(p_small)
    x_small = np.linspace(0,1,m)
    x_large = np.linspace(0,1,n)
    # cdf at small endpoints 0..1. Use np.interp(x_large, x_small, cs_small)
    c_large = np.interp(x_large, x_small, cs_small)
    c_large[0]=0; c_large[-1]=1
    p_large=np.diff(c_large)
    p_large=np.clip(p_large,0,None); normalize.
```
This preserves integral. If p_small smooth, good.

Now, final exact local search with block multipliers. Suppose best_p found. We can do:
```
def local_block_search(p, budget, rng):
  p=p.copy(); best_y=...
  while time:
    width = rng.integers(1,max(2,n//50), n//5?)
    start=rng.integers(0,n-width)
    # propose multiplier r = exp(U(-1,1)*0.2) or random from [0.2,5]
    q=p.copy(); q[start:start+width]*=r; normalize; y=get_y(q); if max<best_y accept
    # occasionally random log noise global gaussian sigma 0.02-0.2
```
This is O(FFT) per attempt. Could find tiny improvements. But if best near optimum, fine.

```
def seed_uniform(n, rng=None): return np.full(n,1.0)/n

def seed_gaussian(n, mu=0.45, sigma=0.35):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   p=np.exp(-0.5*((x-mu)/sigma)**2)
   # clip away from boundaries? keep positive
   p[:]=np.maximum(p,1e-300); normalize

def seed_power(n, a=0.5, side=1):
   x=np.linspace(1e-12,1,n)
   if side==0: p=x**a  # increasing
   else: p=(1-x)**a
```

We can implement a "spectral" dense seed: Take desired flat y (all 0.5), compute P = sqrt(DFT(y)) with phases to make IDFT nonnegative? We can solve phase problem via iterative: Start with p positive, compute P=FFT(p). We want P² ≈ DFT(flat). Update p ← |IDFT( sqrt_target * exp(2i arg(P)) )|? This is like spectral factorization to flatten convolution squares (not power). Let's derive. In DFT domain, convolution q has DFT Q=P² (no conjugate). If we target Q_k = T_k (DFT of flat sequence), we need find P with P²≈T_k and p inverse nonnegative. We can iteratively:
- Given p, compute P=FFT(p).
- Desired phase for P: Since T_k known and may be real with sign/phase. Let P_des_k = sqrt(T_k) where sqrt branch chosen to have P_{-k}=conj(P_k) for real p, and also maybe align with current P to improve. Then p_new = |IDFT(P_des)| (take absolute to enforce nonnegative) normalized.
This is a fixed-point algorithm (like Golay complementary sequences / spectral factorization). It might find a nonnegative p with flat q. This is compelling! We can implement using DFT length L≥n? For full convolution length 2n-1, choose L≥2n-1 (or 3n? For product P² in frequency with linear convolution no wrap). We can target Q desired as DFT of uniform y? Since p length n zero-padded to L. Desired q length 2n-1 with values maybe target M constant? To minimize max, choose q_k = constant = n/(2n-1) (sum y) or maybe constant plus taper? We can set desired y_target = 1.0? Wait y = n c sum n. Sum y=n. If we target y constant = n/(2n-1)≈0.5 over 2n-1. Then DFT Q_target. Algorithm:
```
L=next pow2 >=2n-1
q_target = np.zeros(L); q_target[:2n-1] = n/(2n-1)  # since y constant
Q = rfft(q_target)
# But Q may have complex values and zeros/negative? It's DFT of nonnegative q, so fine.
# We need P such that P*P = Q. But P from real p length n satisfies P[L-k]=conj(P[k]). Then Q should be self: Q[L-k]=? For real p, Q=P² => Q[L-k]=conj(P[k])²=conj(Q[k])? DFT of q length 2n-1 not symmetric (q constant symmetric? q_target constant over 0..2n-2 is symmetric: q_r=q_{2n-2-r}. DFT of symmetric sequence around (2n-2)/2 is real with linear phase: Q_k = C e^{-iπ(2n-2)k/L} times sinc. Thus Q_{L-k}=C e^{iπ(-?)}? conj? yes Q real up to phase, Q_{L-k}=conj(Q[k]). Good.
# Choose sqrt: P_k = sqrt(abs(Q_k)) * exp(0.5 i angle(Q_k)). For k=0, positive. For k>0, choose branch. To ensure real p, set P_{L-k}=conj(P_k).
# Compute p = ifft(P). It may have signed values; take abs (or positive part) to keep nonnegative.

```
This is analogous to "uniform power sequence" but uses square not modulus. It might converge to a nonnegative solution if exists. Because taking abs imposes positivity. We can run iterations from different initial p/phases. This is a new algorithm likely to flatten q strongly. Let's include!

Specifically:
```
def spectral_flat_iter(p, L, iterations, mix=0.5, treat_abs=True):
   n=p.size
   L = next power >=2n-1 (can be same as y L but not 3n? For FFT product length L >= 2n-1. For Q_target DFT length L, P length L. p zero padded L. Use rfft.)
   q_target = np.zeros(L); val = n/(2n-1)*np.ones(2n-1) ; Q = rfft(q_target)
   for it:
      P = rfft(p, L)
      # If Q has zeros, P=0; avoid divide. Use phase alignment: P_new = abs(Q)^0.5 * exp(i * angle(Q)/2). But which branch? angle half.
      mag = np.sqrt(np.abs(Q))
      phase = 0.5*np.angle(Q)
      P_new = mag * np.exp(1j*phase)
      # Enforce symmetry by using full complex? rfft stores half; irfft enforces conjugate automatically if we provide half with rfft-like? For irfft, input X must satisfy conjugate symmetry; if we set only half arbitrarily, irfft assumes Hermitian: for k>0, negative conjugate; it doesn't check. So P_half above ok.
      p_new_complex = np.fft.irfft(P_new, L)[:n]  # should be real
      # enforce nonnegative: p_new = np.abs(p_new_complex) maybe or positive part
      if pos_part: p_new = np.maximum(p_new,0)
      normalize; mix: p = (1-mix)*p + mix*p_new; normalize
   return p
```
But Q target from constant y may have Q_k with angle; half-angle may produce P with p not sparse. Taking abs may destroy symmetry. We can try. If p remains positive and q flat, great.

We need compute the score of such p; even if p has tiny negative part removed, convolution may still lower. This "spectral root + positive part" may be very effective. Let's include as a seed generator and local optimizer.

However, q_target constant over 0..2n-2 not symmetric? It is symmetric. Its DFT real? Yes. The half-angle branch may produce signs. Taking abs may yield a plausible p. Multiple iterations with mix may converge.

Could also target y not exactly constant but a smooth desired profile that is feasible (e.g., constant with zero edges? Constant already zero endpoints? constant includes endpoints =0.5, but actual y endpoints can be near0 if p endpoints small; constant target might be infeasible, but algorithm may approximate.

We can choose a target y that is flat but with edges slightly lower: y_k = (1-δ) constant for all? Sum must n. Maybe y_k = constant * (0.5 + 0.5 cos(π k/(2n-1)))? That's triangular? No.

Spectral iteration variants:
- Target uniform y (average 0.5).
- Start from uniform/gaussian; iterate 100-500 steps, mix 0.1-0.5, use abs or positive part.
- Then run gradient Adam on result.

mag = np.sqrt(np.abs(Q))
phase_base = 0.5*np.angle(Q)
for each trial:
   # random sign flips on positive frequencies: sign = rng.choice([-1,1], size=len(mag)); for idx0 sign[0]=1

def spectral_project(p, L, n, iterations, relax=0.5, use_abs=True):
   q_target avg; Q_target=rfft(q)
   for it in range(iter):
       P= rfft(p, L)
       P2=P*P
       # avoid zero: where abs(P2)>eps, factor = (Q_target/P2)^0.5; where zero, factor=1 or use sign
       factor = np.empty_like(P2, complex)
       mask=abs(P2)>1e-12
       factor[mask] = np.sqrt(Q_target[mask]/P2[mask])
       factor[~mask] = 1.0
       P_new = P * relax + factor * (1-relax)? Hmm factor should multiply P to reach target, but applying full factor each iter may overshoot. Better P_target = factor * P; then P = (1-r)*P + r*P_target.
       p_complex = irfft(P, L)[:n]
       p2 = np.maximum(p_complex,0) if use_pos else np.abs(p_complex)
       p2 += small; normalize
       p = (1-step)*p + step*p2; normalize
   return p
```

```
def next_power_of_two(x):
    if x <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()
```

```

# floor zeros? Keep zeros allowed. For optimizer, add small? Return p.

```

```
def optimize_gradient(p0, L, budget_s, gamma_start=5.0, gamma_end=40.0, lr_start=0.08, lr_end=0.01, flat_start=0.5, flat_end=0.1, restart_warn=0.05, print_tag=''):
   n=p0.size; avg=n/(2*n-1)
   p=normalize p; p += 1e-15? maybe
   z=np.log(p + 1e-300)
   z -= np.mean(z)
   m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); it=0
   best_p=p.copy(); best_y=np.inf; best_score=np.inf
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s
   last_print=0; last_exact=0; exact_calls=0
   while time.monotonic() < deadline:
      tfrac = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(1e-6,budget_s))
      gamma = gamma_start + (gamma_end-gamma_start)*tfrac
      lr = lr_start + (lr_end-lr_start)*tfrac
      flat = flat_start + (flat_end-flat_start)*tfrac
      y=get_y(p,L)
      cur=float(y.max())
      if cur < best_y - 1e-12:
         best_y=cur; best_p=p.copy()
         # exact eval if improvement >1e-4 and calls<50
      Wmax=softmax_weights(y,gamma)
      W = Wmax + flat*(y-avg)
      S = hankel_grad(p,W,L)
      g = 2.0*n*S
      mu = float(np.dot(p,g))
      grad = n * p * (g - mu)
      # optional clip grad
      # Adam
      it +=1
      bc1=0.9; bc2=0.999
      m = (1-bc1)*grad + bc1*m
      v = (1-bc2)*grad*grad + bc2*v
      mhat = m/(1-bc1**it)
      vhat = v/(1-bc2**it)
      z = z - lr * mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
      # keep z finite and centered
      z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
      # Maybe if range too large, clip
      zmax = np.max(z)
      if zmax > 100: z -= 100  # no, use relative
      # To avoid all z drift, subtract mean
      z -= np.mean(z)
      # If range >200, rescale z? Could clip to [-100,100]
      z = np.clip(z, -100.0, 100.0)
      p = softmax_stable(z)
      # optional exact
      elapsed=...
      if elapsed-last_print>5: print(...)
      # Reset if stagnation?
      if cur > best_y + 0.1 and (it % 200 == 0): p=best_p; z=np.log(p)+?; m=v=0
   # At end, maybe p best; check exact best_p
   return best_p,best_y
```

def local_perturb_search(p, L, budget_s, rng, step_min=0.01, step_max=0.1):
   p=normalize_p(p); n=p.size
   y=get_y(p,L); best_y=float(y.max()); best_p=p.copy()
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
   iter=0
   while time<deadline:
      # choose type
      if iter % 3 ==0:
         cand=p.copy()
         width=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n//20), n//4+1))
         start_idx=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
         # random multiplier from lognormal or uniform
         mult=np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.2))  # local block change small
         cand[start_idx:start_idx+width] *= mult
      else:
         cand=p.copy()
         # global log noise: z=log(cand+eps)+sigma*normal
         z=np.log(cand+1e-300)
         sigma = 0.02 + 0.05*rng.random()
         z += rng.normal(0, sigma, n)
         cand = softmax_stable(z)
      cand=normalize_p(cand)
      y=get_y(cand,L); ym=float(y.max())
      if ym < best_y - 1e-12:
          best_y=ym; best_p=cand; p=cand
      elif ym < best_y + 0.02 and rng.random()<0.2:
          p=cand  # simulated accept small worse to escape
      iter+=1
   return best_p,best_y
```
Need ensure width range valid. `rng.integers(low, high)` high exclusive; if n//20 <1? use max. For n 6121 fine.

`make_spectral_candidate`:
```
def make_spectral_trial(P_root, L, n, rng, sign_density=0.5, noise_sigma=0.0, mode='abs'):
    P = P_root.copy()
    # random signs on positive frequencies (excluding DC)
    if sign_density >0 and P.size>1:
       r = rng.random(P.size)
       signs = np.where(r < sign_density, -1.0, 1.0).astype(complex)
       signs[0]=1.0
       P = P * signs
    if noise_sigma>0:
       phases = rng.normal(0, noise_sigma, P.size)
       P = P * np.exp(1j*phases)
    arr = np.fft.irfft(P, L)
    p = arr[:n]
    if mode=='abs': p=np.abs(p)
    else: p=np.maximum(p,0.0)
    return normalize_p(p)
```
Maybe `P_root` length L/2+1. `np.angle` sqrt? `np.sqrt` of complex Q. Good.

def spectral_project(p, P_root, L, budget_s, relax=0.1, mode='abs'):
    n=p.size; p=normalize_p(p)
    best_p=p; best_y=inf
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
       P=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
       # blend toward P_root with relax
       P = (1.0 - relax) * P + relax * P_root
       arr=np.fft.irfft(P, L)
       pnew=arr[:n]
       if mode=='abs': pnew=np.abs(pnew)
       else: pnew=np.maximum(pnew,0.0)
       pnew=normalize_p(pnew)
       p = (1.0 - relax) * p + relax * pnew
       p=normalize_p(p)
       y=get_y(p,L); cur=2*float(y.max())
       if cur < best_y: best=...
       it++
    return best_p,best_y
```

def select_best(pool, k):
    best=[]
    for item in pool:
       # insert sorted by score manually
       inserted=False
       for j, existing in enumerate(best):
          if item[0] < existing[0]:
             best.insert(j,item); inserted=True; break
       if not inserted and len(best)<k: best.append(item)
       if len(best)>k: pop last? Actually best length k, if item not better than all, ignore.
    if len(best)>k: del best[k:]
    return best

```
def seed_global(n):
    try:
       h = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
       return None
    if h is None: return None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(h,dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
       return None
    arr=arr.reshape(-1)
    arr=np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    if arr.size==0 or arr.sum()<=0: return None
    if arr.size == n:
       p=arr/arr.sum()
    else:
       p=resample_p(arr, n)
    return p
```

Let's code `make_spectral_trial` carefully:
```
def make_spectral_trial(P_root, L, n, rng, sign_density=0.5, noise_sigma=0.0, mode="abs"):
    P = P_root.copy()
    if P.size > 1 and sign_density > 0:
        r = rng.random(P.size)
        signs = np.where(r < sign_density, -1.0, 1.0)
        signs[0]=1.0
        P = P * signs.astype(np.complex128)
    if noise_sigma > 0:
        phase = rng.normal(0.0, noise_sigma, P.size).astype(np.float64)
        P = P * np.exp(1j*phase)
    P = np.where(np.isfinite(P), P, 0.0+0.0j)
    out = np.fft.irfft(P, L)
    p = out[:n]
    if mode == "abs": p = np.abs(p)
    else: p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    return normalize_p(p, fallback_n=n)
```
`normalize_p` fallback_n? If sum zero, return uniform of len p. okay.

def spectral_project(p, P_root, L, budget_s, relax=0.25, mode="abs"):
   start...
   best_p=p; best_y=inf
   it=0
   while time<deadline:
       tfrac = min(1,(elapsed)/budget_s)
       r = relax * (1.0 - 0.5*tfrac) + 0.05*tfrac
       P=rfft(p,L)
       P = (1-r)*P + r*P_root
       arr=irfft(P,L)[:n]
       pnew = abs/pos
       p = (1-r)*p + r*pnew
       p=normalize
       y=get_y; cur=2*max
       update best
       it+=1
   return best
```

def select_best(pool, k):

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * n * max(full convolution(a,a)) / (sum a)^2

The code works with probability normalizations (sum a = 1), so the score is
2 * max(n * convolution(a,a)).  It combines several heuristics:

  * deterministic parametric seeds (uniform, Gaussian, exponential, powers,
    reciprocal, random-phase CAZAC-like, global seed),
  * projected spectral factorization around the ideal flat convolution
    spectrum,
  * smoothed-max/L2 projected gradient optimization on the simplex,
  * randomized local perturbations,
  * exact scoring with evaluate_sequence() before returning.

The final sequence is nonnegative, length about 6121 (or the existing
global length if suitable), and scale-free normalized to sum 1.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def next_power_of_two(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_p(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(p.shape, 1.0 / p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    p = p / s
    p[np.abs(p) < 1e-300] = 0.0
    return p


def get_y(p, L):
    """Return y = n * full_convolution(p,p), where p is a probability vector."""
    p = normalize_p(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None or L < 2 * n - 1:
        L = next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, L)
    y = conv[:2 * n - 1] * float(n)
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    np.maximum(y, 0.0, out=y)
    return y


def score_y(p, L):
    y = get_y(p, L)
    if y.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    return 2.0 * float(np.max(y))


def softmax_weights(y, gamma):
    """Softmax weights approximating the maximum of y."""
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if y.size == 0:
        return y
    y = np.where(np.isfinite(y), y, 0.0)
    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, 10.0)
    if gamma <= 0.0:
        return np.full(y.shape, 1.0 / y.size, dtype=np.float64)
    ay = gamma * y
    ay -= float(np.max(ay))
    w = np.exp(ay)
    s = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(y.shape, 1.0 / y.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return w / s


def hankel_grad(p, W, L):
    """
    For W indexed 0..2n-2, return S_j = sum_i p_i W_{i+j}.
    This is the gradient kernel of sum_i,j p_i p_j W_{i+j}.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    n = int(p.size)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    W = np.asarray(W, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if W.size != m:
        tmp = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        k = min(W.size, m)
        if k > 0:
            tmp[:k] = W[:k]
        W = tmp
    if L is None or L < 3 * n - 2:
        L = next_power_of_two(3 * n - 2)
    pr = p[::-1]
    fa = np.fft.rfft(pr, L)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(W, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, L)
    return c[n - 1:2 * n - 1].copy()


def softmax_stable(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if z.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, -700.0)
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    e = np.exp(np.clip(z, -745.0, 0.0))
    s = float(np.sum(e))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(z.shape, 1.0 / z.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def seed_uniform(n):
    return np.full(int(n), 1.0, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)


def seed_gaussian(n, mu, sigma):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    sigma = max(1e-6, float(sigma))
    p = np.exp(-0.5 * ((x - float(mu)) / sigma) ** 2)
    p = p + 1e-300
    return normalize_p(p)


def seed_exponential(n, lam, side):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    if side == 0:
        p = np.exp(-float(lam) * x)
    else:
        p = np.exp(-float(lam) * (1.0 - x))
    p = p + 1e-300
    return normalize_p(p)


def seed_power(n, alpha, side):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.linspace(1e-12, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    if side == 0:
        p = x ** float(alpha)
    else:
        p = (1.0 - x) ** float(alpha)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    if np.sum(p) <= 0.0:
        return seed_uniform(n)
    return normalize_p(p)


def seed_reciprocal(n, B):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    B = max(1e-6, float(B))
    p = 1.0 / (B + x)
    return normalize_p(p)


def seed_ca_like(n, L, rng):
    """Random real spectrum; take absolute value of inverse transform."""
    n = int(n)
    L = next_power_of_two(max(1, 2 * n - 1))
    half = L // 2 + 1
    ph = 1.0 + 1j * 0.0
    ph[1:] = np.exp(1j * rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi, half - 1))
    half[-1] = float(half[-1].real)
    out = np.fft.irfft(half, L)
    p = np.abs(out[:n])
    return normalize_p(p)


def resample_p(p, n):
    n = int(n)
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    p = np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    if p.size == 0 or float(np.sum(p)) <= 0.0:
        return seed_uniform(n)
    p = p / float(np.sum(p))
    if p.size == n:
        return p
    if p.size == 1:
        return seed_uniform(n)
    xs = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, p.size, dtype=np.float64)
    cs = np.cumsum(p)
    cs = cs / cs[-1]
    xd = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, dtype=np.float64)
    cd = np.interp(xd, xs, cs)
    cd[0] = 0.0
    cd[-1] = 1.0
    q = np.diff(cd)
    q = np.maximum(q, 0.0)
    if np.sum(q) <= 0.0:
        return seed_uniform(n)
    q = q / float(np.sum(q))
    return q


def seed_global(n):
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        return None
    if h is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    except Exception:
        return None
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == 0 or float(np.sum(arr)) <= 0.0:
        return None
    if arr.size == int(n):
        return arr / float(np.sum(arr))
    return resample_p(arr, int(n))


def choose_target_length():
    n = 6121
    try:
        h = height_sequence_1
        if h is not None:
            m = len(h)
            if 512 <= m <= 7000:
                n = int(m)
            elif m > 7000:
                n = 6121
            elif m > 0:
                n = 4096
    except Exception:
        pass
    if n < 256:
        n = 2048
    if n > 7000:
        n = 6121
    return int(n)


def precompute_spectral_root(n, L):
    """Spectrum whose square is the DFT of a constant target convolution."""
    n = int(n)
    if L is None:
        L = next_power_of_two(max(1, 2 * n - 1))
    length = 2 * n - 1
    q = np.zeros(int(L), dtype=np.float64)
    if length > 0:
        q[:length] = float(n) / float(length)
    Q = np.fft.rfft(q)
    P = np.sqrt(Q.astype(np.complex128))
    if L % 2 == 0:
        nyq = Q[-1].real
        if nyq < 0.0:
            P[-1] = 0.0 + 0.0j
        else:
            P[-1] = np.sqrt(float(nyq)) + 0.0j
    P = np.where(np.isfinite(P), P, 0.0 + 0.0j)
    return P, Q


def make_spectral_trial(P_root, L, n, rng, sign_density=0.5, noise_sigma=0.0, mode="abs"):
    n = int(n)
    P = P_root.copy()
    if P.size > 1:
        if sign_density > 0.0:
            r = rng.random(P.size)
            signs = np.where(r < sign_density, -1.0, 1.0).astype(np.complex128)
            signs[0] = 1.0 + 0.0j
            P = P * signs
        if noise_sigma > 0.0:
            phase = rng.normal(0.0, float(noise_sigma), P.size).astype(np.float64)
            phase[0] = 0.0
            P = P * np.exp(1j * phase)
    if L % 2 == 0 and P.size > 0:
        P[-1] = float(np.real(P[-1])) + 0.0j
    P = np.where(np.isfinite(P), P, 0.0 + 0.0j)
    out = np.fft.irfft(P, int(L))
    p = out[:n]
    if mode == "abs":
        p = np.abs(p)
    else:
        p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    return normalize_p(p)


def spectral_project(p, P_root, L, budget_s, relax=0.25, mode="abs"):
    p = normalize_p(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        y = get_y(p, L)
        return p, 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(budget_s)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_y = np.inf
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        tfrac = 0.0 if elapsed <= 0.0 else min(1.0, elapsed / max(1e-6, float(budget_s)))
        r = (1.0 - tfrac) * float(relax) + tfrac * max(0.03, 0.5 * float(relax))
        P = np.fft.rfft(p, int(L))
        P = (1.0 - r) * P + r * P_root
        if L % 2 == 0 and P.size > 0:
            P[-1] = float(np.real(P[-1])) + 0.0j
        out = np.fft.irfft(P, int(L))
        p_new = out[:n]
        if mode == "abs":
            p_new = np.abs(p_new)
        else:
            p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 0.0)
        p_new = normalize_p(p_new)
        p = (1.0 - r) * p + r * p_new
        p = normalize_p(p)
        y = get_y(p, L)
        cur = 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
        if np.isfinite(cur) and cur < best_y:
            best_y = cur
            best_p = p.copy()
    return best_p, best_y


def optimize_gradient(p0, L, budget_s,
                      gamma_start=5.0, gamma_end=40.0,
                      lr_start=0.06, lr_end=0.01,
                      flat_start=0.5, flat_end=0.1,
                      tag="opt"):
    p = normalize_p(p0)
    n = int(p.size)
    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        y = get_y(p, L)
        return p, 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
    if L is None or L < 3 * n - 2:
        L = next_power_of_two(3 * n - 2)

    z = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    z -= float(np.mean(z))
    m1 = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v2 = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_y = np.inf
    it = 0
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(budget_s)
    last_print = 0.0
    avg = float(n) / float(2 * n - 1)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        tfrac = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1e-6, float(budget_s)))
        gamma = float(gamma_start) + (float(gamma_end) - float(gamma_start)) * tfrac
        lr = float(lr_start) + (float(lr_end) - float(lr_start)) * tfrac
        flat = float(flat_start) + (float(flat_end) - float(flat_start)) * tfrac

        y = get_y(p, L)
        cur = 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
        if np.isfinite(cur) and cur < best_y:
            best_y = cur
            best_p = p.copy()

        if elapsed - last_print > 10.0:
            print(f"[{tag}] n={n} t={elapsed:.1f}s fft_score={cur:.6f} best={best_y:.6f}", flush=True)
            last_print = elapsed

        # Smooth max plus L2 flattening.
        Wmax = softmax_weights(y, gamma)
        dev = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64) - avg
        dev = np.clip(dev, -5.0, 5.0)
        W = Wmax + flat * dev
        W = np.where(np.isfinite(W), W, 0.0)

        S = hankel_grad(p, W, L)
        g = 2.0 * float(n) * S

        # This is the exact gradient on the simplex simplex z=log(p).
        mu = float(np.dot(p, g))
        grad = float(n) * p * (g - mu)
        grad = np.clip(np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0), -1e6, 1e6)

        it += 1
        b1 = 0.9
        b2 = 0.999
        m1 = (1.0 - b1) * grad + b1 * m1
        v2 = (1.0 - b2) * (grad * grad) + b2 * v2
        mhat = m1 / (1.0 - b1 ** it)
        vhat = v2 / (1.0 - b2 ** it)
        step = mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-8)
        z = z - lr * step

        # Keep logits finite and centered.
        z = np.where(np.isfinite(z), z, 0.0)
        z -= float(np.mean(z))
        z = np.clip(z, -80.0, 80.0)
        p = softmax_stable(z)

        # If things get badly stuck, return to the best point.
        if it > 50 and it % 200 == 0 and np.isfinite(best_y) and cur > best_y + 0.25:
            p = best_p.copy()
            z = np.log(p + 1e-300)
            z -= float(np.mean(z))
            m1.fill(0.0)
            v2.fill(0.0)

    y = get_y(best_p, L)
    return best_p, 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf


def local_perturb_search(p0, L, budget_s, rng):
    p = normalize_p(p0)
    n = int(p.size)
    if budget_s <= 0.0:
        y = get_y(p, L)
        return p, 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + float(budget_s)
    y = get_y(p, L)
    best_y = 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
    best_p = p.copy()
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = p.copy()
        if it % 3 == 0:
            upper = max(2, min(n // 5, 4096))
            lower = max(1, min(4, upper))
            width = int(rng.integers(lower, upper))
            st = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
            mult = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.20)))
            cand[st:st + width] *= mult
        elif it % 3 == 1:
            z = np.log(cand + 1e-300)
            sigma = 0.02 + 0.08 * float(rng.random())
            z += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
            cand = softmax_stable(z)
        else:
            st = int(rng.integers(0, n))
            half = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, n // 80)))
            left = max(0, st - half)
            right = min(n, st + half + 1)
            mult = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.35)))
            cand[left:right] *= mult

        cand = normalize_p(cand)
        y = get_y(cand, L)
        cur = 2.0 * float(np.max(y)) if y.size else np.inf
        if np.isfinite(cur) and cur < best_y - 1e-12:
            best_y = cur
            best_p = cand.copy()
            p = cand.copy()
        elif np.isfinite(cur) and cur < best_y + 0.03 and rng.random() < 0.15:
            p = cand.copy()
        it += 1

    return best_p, best_y


def add_candidate(pool, score, p, max_size=20):
    if p is None:
        return
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
    if p.size == 0:
        return
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return
    p = normalize_p(p)
    pool.append([float(score), p])
    if len(pool) > max_size:
        idx = 0
        for i in range(1, len(pool)):
            if pool[i][0] > pool[idx][0]:
                idx = i
        pool.pop(idx)


def select_best(pool, k):
    res = []
    for item in pool:
        res.append(item)
        j = len(res) - 1
        while j > 0 and res[j][0] < res[j - 1][0]:
            res[j], res[j - 1] = res[j - 1], res[j]
            j -= 1
        if len(res) > k:
            del res[k:]
    return res


def exact_candidate(p):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(p.tolist()))
    except Exception:
        return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400.0, **kwargs):
    """Return the best nonnegative sequence found within budget_s seconds."""
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 25.0)
    if total_budget <= 1.0:
        return seed_uniform(4096).tolist()

    n = choose_target_length()
    L = next_power_of_two(3 * n - 2)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)

    print(f"[propose] start n={n} L={L} budget={total_budget:.1f}s", flush=True)

    pool = []
    uniform = seed_uniform(n)
    add_candidate(pool, score_y(uniform, L), uniform, 20)

    # Deterministic seeds.
    seeds = [uniform]
    for mu, sig in [(0.30, 0.30), (0.35, 0.35), (0.40, 0.40), (0.45, 0.50), (0.35, 0.55)]:
        seeds.append(seed_gaussian(n, mu, sig))
    for lam in [0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0]:
        seeds.append(seed_exponential(n, lam, 0))
        seeds.append(seed_exponential(n, lam, 1))
    for alpha in [0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.0, 1.5]:
        seeds.append(seed_power(n, alpha, 0))
        seeds.append(seed_power(n, alpha, 1))
    for B in [0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0]:
        seeds.append(seed_reciprocal(n, B))
    for _ in range(6):
        seeds.append(seed_ca_like(n, L, rng))
    gseed = seed_global(n)
    if gseed is not None:
        seeds.append(gseed)
        seeds.append(gseed[::-1])

    for sp in seeds:
        try:
            sc = score_y(sp, L)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                add_candidate(pool, sc, sp, 20)
        except Exception:
            pass

    best_item = select_best(pool, 1)
    if best_item:
        print(f"[propose] after deterministic seeds best_fft={best_item[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Spectral factorization trials around the ideal flat convolution spectrum.
    P_root, Q_root = precompute_spectral_root(n, L)
    trial_budget = min(90.0, max(15.0, total_budget * 0.035))
    deadline = time.monotonic() + trial_budget
    trial = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and trial < 6000:
        # Vary branch signs, small phase noise, and projection mode.
        sign_density = [0.0, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.65][trial % 5]
        noise = [0.0, 0.05, 0.15, 0.30, 0.0][trial % 5]
        mode = "abs" if (trial % 2 == 0) else "pos"
        p = make_spectral_trial(P_root, L, n, rng,
                                sign_density=sign_density,
                                noise_sigma=noise,
                                mode=mode)
        sc = score_y(p, L)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            add_candidate(pool, sc, p, 20)
        trial += 1

    best_item = select_best(pool, 1)
    if best_item:
        print(f"[propose] after spectral trials best_fft={best_item[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Project/refine a few good spectral candidates.
    top = select_best(pool, 4)
    proj_each = min(35.0, max(5.0, total_budget * 0.018))
    for ix, item in enumerate(top):
        if time.monotonic() - t0 > total_budget * 0.30:
            break
        p0 = item[1]
        relax = 0.20 + 0.05 * float(ix)
        mode = "abs" if (ix % 2 == 0) else "pos"
        try:
            p1, sc1 = spectral_project(p0, P_root, L, proj_each, relax=relax, mode=mode)
            add_candidate(pool, sc1, p1, 20)
            print(f"[propose] spectral project {ix}: score={sc1:.6f}", flush=True)
        except Exception:
            pass

    best_item = select_best(pool, 1)
    if best_item:
        print(f"[propose] after spectral projection best_fft={best_item[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Smoothed-max + L2 gradient optimization from strongest candidates.
    top = select_best(pool, 3)
    configs = [
        (3.0, 25.0, 0.08, 0.015, 1.0, 0.20, "flat1"),
        (10.0, 55.0, 0.05, 0.010, 0.25, 0.08, "peak1"),
        (30.0, 120.0, 0.02, 0.004, 0.05, 0.0, "peak2"),
    ]
    runs_total = max(1, len(top) * len(configs))
    reserved_tail = min(350.0, max(120.0, total_budget * 0.15))
    for cfg in configs:
        g0, g1, lr0, lr1, f0, f1, tag = cfg
        top = select_best(pool, 3)
        for ix, item in enumerate(top):
            elapsed = time.monotonic() - t0
            remaining = total_budget - elapsed
            if remaining <= reserved_tail + 10.0:
                break
            alloc = min(260.0, max(8.0, (remaining - reserved_tail) / max(1, runs_total)))
            if alloc < 3.0:
                break
            p0 = item[1]
            try:
                p1, sc1 = optimize_gradient(
                    p0, L, alloc,
                    gamma_start=g0, gamma_end=g1,
                    lr_start=lr0, lr_end=lr1,
                    flat_start=f0, flat_end=f1,
                    tag=f"{tag}[{ix}]"
                )
                add_candidate(pool, sc1, p1, 20)
                print(f"[propose] grad {tag} cand={ix}: score={sc1:.6f}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass
        runs_total = max(1, runs_total - max(1, len(top)))

    best_item = select_best(pool, 1)
    if best_item:
        print(f"[propose] after gradient optimization best_fft={best_item[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Localrandomized hill-climbing on the current best.
    tail_left = total_budget - (time.monotonic() - t0)
    local_budget = min(140.0, max(20.0, tail_left - 80.0))
    if local_budget > 20.0:
        top = select_best(pool, 1)
        if top:
            p0, sc0 = top[0]
            try:
                p1, sc1 = local_perturb_search(p0, L, local_budget, rng)
                add_candidate(pool, sc1, p1, 20)
                print(f"[propose] local search: score={sc1:.6f}", flush=True)
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Exact scoring of the best several candidates.
    cands = select_best(pool, min(8, len(pool)))
    if not cands:
        return uniform.tolist()

    exact_best_p = cands[0][1]
    exact_best_score = np.inf
    exact_deadline = t0 + total_budget

    # Always include uniform and the best FFT candidate as fallbacks.
    check = []
    seen_shapes = []
    for item in cands:
        check.append(item[1])
    check.append(uniform)

    for p in check:
        if time.monotonic() >= exact_deadline - 3.0:
            break
        try:
            sc = exact_candidate(p)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < exact_best_score:
                exact_best_score = sc
                exact_best_p = normalize_p(p)
                print(f"[propose] exact score={sc:.6f}", flush=True)
        except Exception:
            pass

    final_p = normalize_p(exact_best_p)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(final_p)) or float(np.sum(final_p)) <= 0.0:
        final_p = uniform

    print(f"[propose] final n={final_p.size} sum={float(np.sum(final_p)):.6f} "
          f"exact_best={exact_best_score if np.isfinite(exact_best_score) else 'n/a'} "
          f"last_fft={cands[0][0]:.6f}", flush=True)

    return final_p.tolist()
```
